Let's think of proving lower bound in comments:
```
# For the final clipped nonnegative array a, b_0 (the central autocorrelation)
# equals sum_i a_i^2. Because for any lag k, b_k = sum_i a_i a_{i+k} <=
# sqrt(sum_i a_i^2 * sum_i a_{i+k}^2) <= sum_i a_i^2. Hence max(b) = b_0.
# Therefore score = 2n sum_i a_i^2 / (sum_i a_i)^2 >= 2 by Cauchy, with equality
# exactly for a constant sequence. Thus the optimum is a constant sequence.
```
This is strong. Note central index? np.convolve full output has b_{n-1}=sum_i a_i a_i. max at that. For any lag, bound. Good.

```
def _parse_args(args, kwargs):
    seed = 20240525
    budget_s = 2400.0
    parent = kwargs.pop('height_sequence', None)
    if 'seed' in kwargs: seed=kwargs['seed']
    if 'budget_s' in kwargs: budget_s=kwargs['budget_s']
    # if first positional is sequence
    if args and isinstance(args[0], (list, np.ndarray)):
        parent = args[0]
        args = args[1:]
    # remaining positionals: maybe seed, budget_s
    if args:
        try: seed = int(args[0])
        except: pass
    if len(args)>1:
        try: budget_s = float(args[1])
        except: pass
    # If seed actually list? handled.
    if parent is None:
        parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    return seed, budget_s, parent
```

```
def _parse_arguments(args, kwargs):
    parent = kwargs.get('height_sequence', None)
    seed = kwargs.get('seed', 2024)
    budget_s = kwargs.get('budget_s', 2400.0)
    positional_types = []
    for a in args:
       if isinstance(a, (list, np.ndarray)):
          parent = a
       elif isinstance(a, (int, float)) and not isinstance(a, bool):
          positional_types.append(a)
    if positional_types:
       if not ('seed' in kwargs): seed = positional_types[0]
       if len(positional_types)>1 and not ('budget_s' in kwargs): budget_s = positional_types[1]
    if parent is None:
       parent = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    return seed, float(budget_s), parent
```

```
"""Inequality-based global solver for the given evaluator.

For the final clipped array a (nonnegative), the full autocorrelation at lag k
satisfies |k| cross term <= sum a_i^2 by Cauchy-Schwarz. Hence max(conv(a,a)) = sum a_i^2,
and score = 2n sum a_i^2/(sum a_i)^2 >= 2, with equality for positive constant sequences.
Therefore any positive constant sequence is a global optimum (subject to floating-point
effects). The program returns a constant sequence and optionally does a tiny empirical
polish over constant scales/lengths to guard against rounding adverse to the constant choice.
"""
import numpy as np
import time

_SEQUENCE_DEFAULT_LENGTH = 2400
_MAX_SCAN_TIME = 30.0
_MAX_SCAN_LENGTH = 16384

def _parse_call_signature(*args, **kwargs):
   ...

def _constant_sequence(n, value):
    n = int(max(1,n))
    value = float(value)
    if not np.isfinite(value) or value <=0 or value >1000: value=1.0
    return [value] * n

def _safe_score(seq):
    try:
       return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
       return np.inf

def _initial_best(parent):
   # choose length
   n = _SELECT length based on parent maybe.
   fallback = _constant_sequence(n,1.0)
   score = _safe_score(fallback)
   if not np.isfinite(score):
        # try smaller
        for m in (1024,128,1): ...
   return seq, score

def _polish_constant_only(budget_s):
   best_seq, best_score = ...
   deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, min(float(budget_s)-2.0, _MAX_SCAN_TIME))
   lengths = ...
   scale sweep
   for n in lengths:
      if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
      for v in scales:
         seq = _constant_sequence(n, v)
         if n == len(best_seq) and v == best_val? skip
         score = _safe_score(seq)
         if np.isfinite(score):
             if score < best_score - 1e-15:
                 best_score = score; best_seq=seq
                 print(...)
   # if no improvement, return initial (length 2400) not something with worse rounding.
   return best_seq if best_score < something? Actually if candidate 2400 value 0.1 score 2.0, keep first. Use tie tolerance; if no improvement return initial.

def propose_candidate(*args, **kwargs):
    seed, budget_s, parent = _parse...
    best_seq, best_score = _initial_best(parent)
    # If budget small, return
    try:
       best_seq, best_score = _polish_constant_only(best_seq, best_score, budget_s)
    except Exception:
       pass
    # final validate
    try:
       seq = best_seq
       if not isinstance(seq, list) or not seq:
           seq = _constant_sequence(_SEQUENCE_DEFAULT_LENGTH,1.0)
       # Clip values to [0,1000] and finite
       out=[]
       for x in seq:
          try: xf=float(x)
          except: xf=1.0
          if not np.isfinite(xf): xf=1.0
          if xf <0: xf=0.0
          if xf >1000: xf=1000.0
          out.append(xf)
       if not out or np.sum([min(1000,max(0,xf)) for xf in out]) <0.01: out=[1.0]*2400
       return out
    except Exception:
       return [1.0]*_SEQUENCE_DEFAULT_LENGTH
```

Let's implement helpers:
```
def _constant_sequence(n, value):
    try:
       n = int(n)
    except Exception:
       n = 2400
    if n < 1: n = 1
    if n > 1000000: n = 2400 # safety
    try:
       v = float(value)
    except Exception:
       v = 1.0
    if not np.isfinite(v): v=1.0
    if v <= 0.0: v=1.0
    if v > 1000.0: v=1000.0
    return [v] * n
```
Should we allow zero? "positive or zero"; but sum must >0.01. If all zeros invalid. Use positive.

```
def _final_validate(seq):
    try:
      if isinstance(seq, list) and seq:
        out=[]
        s=0.0
        for x in seq:
           try: xf=float(x)
           except: xf=1.0
           if not np.isfinite(xf): xf=1.0
           if xf <0: xf=0.0
           if xf >1000: xf=1000.0
           if xf != 0.0: s += xf
           out.append(xf)
        if len(out)>0 and s >=0.01:
           return out
      return _constant_sequence(2400,1.0)
    except Exception:
      return _constant_sequence(2400,1.0)
```

```
def _parse_call_signature(*args, **kwargs):
    seed = 123456789
    budget_s = 2400.0
    parent = kwargs.get("height_sequence", None)
    if "seed" in kwargs:
        seed = kwargs["seed"]
    if "budget_s" in kwargs:
        budget_s = kwargs["budget_s"]
    numeric_positionals = []
    for a in args:
        if isinstance(a, (list, np.ndarray, tuple)):
            if parent is None:
                parent = a
        elif isinstance(a, (int, float)) and not isinstance(a, bool):
            numeric_positionals.append(a)
    if numeric_positionals:
        if "seed" not in kwargs:
            try: seed = int(numeric_positionals[0])
            except Exception: pass
        if len(numeric_positionals) > 1:
            if "budget_s" not in kwargs:
                try: budget_s = float(numeric_positionals[1])
                except Exception: pass
    if not np.isfinite(float(budget_s)): budget_s=2400.0
    if budget_s <0: budget_s=0.0
    return seed, budget_s, parent
```

```
       # If exactly one positional numeric and not plausible seed? Could be 100.0. We can treat if len(args)==1 and isinstance(args[0], float): budget=args[0]
       # If len(args)>=2 and last numeric: budget=args[-1]

       if nums:

   if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or budget_s<0: budget_s=2400.0
```

Maybe the "Target upper bound 1.5027" suggests there is a way under actual eval, and our proof missing something. Let's not dismiss too quickly. Let's attempt to disprove lower bound by considering that `np.convolve(sequence, sequence)` is not autocorrelation with same orientation? For sequence a length n, np.convolve(a,a) central coefficient = sum_i a_i a_{n-1-i}? Wait! Important! I may have mis-indexed. The full convolution of a with a: output c[k] = sum_i a[i] a[k-i], where 0<=i<n,0<=k-i<n. The central lag k=n-1 is sum_i a[i] a[n-1-i], which is **cross-**correlation, not sum of squares! Sum of squares appears at k=0: c[0]=a[0]^2? Wait check np.convolve([a0,a1,a2],[a0,a1,a2]) gives [a0^2, a0a1, a0a2+a1^2, a1a2, a2^2]. The middle coefficient for odd length includes a1^2 plus cross terms. The sum of squares is NOT a single coefficient except for n=1! I made a huge mistake. I confused convolution with autocorrelation (correlate). Algorithm (Frank-Wolfe-like):
1. Initialize f nonnegative, normalize sum =1 or sqrt(2n). M = max(f*f).
2. Solve LP: max sum g s.t. g>=0 and (f*g)_k <= M for all k.
   - Since f normalized sum=1, M around 1/n. g variables n. Rescale h = g / (sum g) to sum=1.
   - Update f <- (1-t) f + t h.
3. Repeat. Nonincreasing M? The text says nonincreasing. Possibly choose t small.
This resembles "linearized" descent for minimizing M? Let's see: For current f, M=||f*f||∞. The LP finds g maximizing total mass such that f*g <= M. Since f itself might not satisfy f*f <= M? Pseudo gradient flow:
```
def fft_convolution(a):
    L = 1 << (2*len(a)-1).bit_length() # for self conv length 2n-1
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)[:2*n-1]
    # due to roundoff negative small; clip? For max, leave; but nonneg expect >0.
    return b
```
Need memory: L for self 4096; for gradient L3=8192. Fine.

```
# To avoid underflow all but peaks zero, okay; if sum underflows? use top K?
z = w.sum()
if z==0 or not finite: uniform over top peak(s)
alpha = w/z
```
Since c around 1, beta up to 100, logits max 0, some down -100. Underflow fine. If b has numerical negative, c negative.

Gradient corr via FFT:
```
def gradient_corr(a, alpha):
    L = 1 << (3*n-2).bit_length()
    ar = a[::-1]
    F1 = rfft(ar, L)
    F2 = rfft(alpha, L)
    corr = irfft(F1*F2, L)[n-1:2*n-1]
    return 2*corr
```
Actually slice length n: indices n-1 to 2n-2 inclusive. In Python `corr[n-1:2*n-1]`. Good.

Objective score from b: `score_fft = 2*n*max(b)` since sum a=1. But FFT b may have tiny negative; max positive. Use exact periodically.


Algorithm plan in code:
```
def propose_candidate(...):
  parse budget_s
  n = choose_length(budget_s)  # maybe 2400 if time, else 512
  start_deadline = time.monotonic() + budget_s
  # Generate initial candidates list
  candidates = []
  # baseline uniform
  candidates.append(uniform(n))
  # one-sided powers
  for alpha in [0.3,0.4,0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6]:
      a = (np.arange(n)+1)^(-alpha); normalize
      # optional tail cutoffs: reduce last 10%,20%
  for tail_f in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3]:
      for tail_scale in [0.0,0.1,0.3,0.5,1.0]:
         a = sqrt profile; last tail of base set *= tail_scale (if 0 -> zeros then normalize)
  # random smooth profiles from sqrt: multiply by exp(smoothed noise)
  candidates evaluate FFT score (target_c = 0.75135; score=2*n*max(b))
  sort by score, take top distinct maybe 5.
  best_exact = exact(top candidate) and also uniform exact.
  # For top candidates, run gradient optimization
  for cand in top:
     a = cand.copy()
     run_local(a, remaining_time / (# restarts), target_c, exact_update)
     exact evaluate final and update
  # Maybe exact local block search around best: perturb small blocks, exact evaluate few.
  return final best scaled
```

```
def make_two_component(n, alpha=0.5, mass_prefix=0.9, support_prefix=0.8):
   arcs = np.zeros(n)
   L = int(support_prefix*n)
   arcs[:L] = (np.arange(L)+1)^-alpha
   # tail uniform on last (1-support_prefix)*n
   if 1-mass_prefix >0:
      R = L + int? maybe all remaining
      arcs[L:] += tail_mass / (n-L) maybe
   normalize
```

```
def prefix_tail_profile(n, alpha=0.5, tail_len=T, prefix_mass=S, prefix_len=n-T):
    a = zeros(n)
    x = np.arange(prefix_len)+1
    pref = x**(-alpha)
    a[:prefix_len] = S * pref / pref.sum()
    if T>0: a[prefix_len:] = (1-S)/T
    if abs(S)>1: allow S<1
```

Grid search function:
```
def profile_prefix_tail(n, alpha, T, S, tail_pos='end'):
   a = np.zeros(n)
   L = n - T
   if L <=0: return uniform
   x = np.arange(1, L+1)
   pref = x ** (-alpha)
   a[:L] = S * pref / pref.sum()
   if T > 0 and S < 1:
       a[L:] = (1.0 - S) / T
   # if S==1, tail zeros
   # ensure nonnegative, sum1
   a = np.minimum(a, None?) 
   a /= a.sum()
   return a
```

Local gradient for final profile (sum1): use FFT self conv. Objective c_max = n*max b. Smooth logistic? We can use top-K subgradient with active peaks. Since we start near good profile, top-K with K e.g. 20-100 and multiplicative updates may reduce max. Use vectorized direct or FFT. We can implement a simple **stochastic active descent**:
```
def optimize_profile(a, time_budget, target_c=0.75135, exact_update_callback):
    a = a.copy(); a /= a.sum()
    best = a.copy(); best_c = self_max_scaled(a)
    lr_factor = 0.1? adaptive
    for iter while time:
       b = conv(a); M = b.max(); c = n*M
       if c < best_c: best_c=c; best=a
       c_scaled = b*n
       # active: top K where K random [1,5,10,20,50,100]
       K = random choice based on violation
       idx = argpartition(c_scaled, -K)[-K:]
       # alpha uniform over idx
       grad = 2*correlation(a, idx/K)  # exact
       # update theta multiplicative with desired_change maybe 0.05
       s = a*grad
       max_s = max(s)
       if max_s>0: step = min(n, target_delta/max_s)
       a_new = a*exp(-step*s); normalize
       # maybe add random noise: a_new *= exp(noise*sigma); normalize
       # accept if self_max_scaled(a_new) < c + small? or use smoothed.
```
Since this is nonconvex, we can allow moves that reduce smoothed objective (softmax). Use top-K direct. But local refinement may be unnecessary if grid+LP already target. We can include a limited version for improvement.

  Given current weights λ, fixing all except λ_i, λ_j with λ_i+λ_j=s, and objective F(λ)=max_k ∑_{p,q} λ_p λ_q C_{k,pq} (where C are convolutions of basis). Now, basis random profiles generation:
```
def _generate_basis(N, lp_u, rng):
    bases=[]
    for alpha in [0.45,0.5,0.55]:
       x=np.arange(1,N+1)
       w=x**(-alpha); w/=w.sum(); bases.append(w)
       bases.append(w[::-1])  # right
    bases.append(np.ones(N)/N)
    if lp_u is not None: bases.append(lp_u)
    # oscillatory
    for f in [1,3,5,7]:
       theta=2*np.pi*f*np.arange(N)/N
       for rho in [0.5,0.9]:
          v = 1.0 + rho*np.cos(theta)
          # ensure nonnegative (rho<=1)
          v=np.clip(v,1e-6,None); v/=v.sum(); bases.append(v)
    # random smooth profiles
    for _ in range(6):
       # start from sqrt profile, add smoothed random noise in log domain
       x=np.arange(N)+1.0
       base=x**(-0.5)
       noise = rng.normal(0,0.3,size=N)
       # smooth by cumulative average or FFT lowpass
       # simple: moving average window 50
       kernel=np.ones(21)/21
       noise=np.convolve(noise,kernel,mode='same')
       a=base*np.exp(noise)
       a=np.clip(a,1e-8,None); a/=a.sum(); bases.append(a)
    # Maybe random sparse atoms profiles:
    for _ in range(4):
       v=np.zeros(N); M=500; idx=rng.integers(0,N,M); v[idx]+=1; v/=v.sum(); bases.append(v)
    return bases
```
Basis size probably ~ (3*2=6)+1+1+8+6+4=26. That's a bit high for mixture construction (26*2400=62k per eval) okay. But random mixture Dirichlet high dim may be too sparse; can use m smaller by selecting top informative? We can keep 20-24. 24 ok.

```
def _fft_length(n): return 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()

def _self_conv_scaled(a, n, L):
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)
    # b length L, take first 2n-1
    # clip small negative? For max no, but for c use max.
    M = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M < 0: M=0.0
    return n*M
```

```
j = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)

cols_c = np.full(m, n)
vals_c = -1.0
rows_all = np.concatenate([rows, rows_c])
cols_all = np.concatenate([cols, cols_c])
vals_all = np.concatenate([vals, vals_c])
A = sp.coo_array((vals_all, (rows_all, cols_all)), shape=(m, n+1)).tocsr()
```

High-dim local gradient top-level functions:
```
def _local_descent(a, n, L, budget_s, target_c, seed):
    a = a.copy(); a /= a.sum()
    best_a = a.copy(); best_c = _fft_score(a)
    sigma = 0.05
    Kchoices = [1,2,5,10,20,50]
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    while time < deadline:
       b = _self_conv_full(a,n,L)
       M = b.max(); c = n*M
       if c < best_c: ...
       cs = np.clip(b,0,None)*n
       # choose K maybe based on c-target
       K = int(rng.choice(Kchoices))
       idx = np.argpartition(cs, -K)[-K:]
       # gradient direct:
       grad = np.zeros(n)
       alpha = 1.0/K
       for k in idx:
           lo = max(0, k - (n-1)); hi = min(n-1, k)
           if hi>=lo:
               p = np.arange(lo, hi+1)
               grad[p] += alpha * a[k - p]
       grad *= 2
       s = a*grad
       max_s = s.max()
       if max_s <=0: break
       desired = 0.05 + 0.2 * min(1, max(0, c-target_c)/(target_c+1e-6))
       step = min(float(n), desired/max_s)
       a_new = a * np.exp(-step*s)
       # add noise
       noise = rng.normal(0, sigma, n)
       a_new *= np.exp(noise*0.1?)
       a_new = np.clip(a_new, 1e-12, None); a_new/=a_new.sum()
       c_new = _fft_score(a_new)
       # accept if c_new <= c + 0.005*target? maybe use smoothed?
       if c_new < best_c + 1e-12 and c_new < c + 0.001:
           a = a_new
           if c_new < best_c: best_c=c_new
       else:
           # try smaller step or noise
```
This may be unstable. Since time budget, maybe skip local if mixture search reaches target. But local can improve. We'll include a conservative local with exact check.

Up per stretching u to final n:
```
def _resample_to_length(x, m):
   x = np.asarray(x, float).ravel()
   if x.size == m: return x
   if x.size ==1: return np.full(m,x[0])/m
   old = np.linspace(0.0, x.size, x.size)
   new = np.linspace(0.0, m, m)
   y = np.interp(new, old, x)
   y = np.clip(y,0,None); s=y.sum(); if s>0: y/=s
   return y
```
If m<x.size, interpolation reduces. Good.

`_fft_score(a, L)`: We need n internally? Score c = n*max(b); pass n or infer length? Use `n = len(a)`. But if a has length maybe. Use helper:
```
def _fft_scaled_max(a, L):
    n = a.size
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)
    if b.size > 2*n-1: b = b[:2*n-1]
    M = np.max(b)
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M < 0: M = 0.0
    return float(n*M)
```
Since a sum1. For mixtures, sum1. Due FFT roundoff max maybe slight. Good.

```
def _add_top(top, c, a, cap=30):
    if not np.isfinite(c): return
    top.append((c,a.copy()))
    if len(top) > cap:
       # remove worst (largest c)
       worst_idx = 0; worst_val = top[0][0]
       for i in range(1,len(top)):
          if top[i][0] > worst_val: worst_idx=i; worst_val=top[i][0]
       top.pop(worst_idx)
```
This grows to cap+1 then trim. Good.

Perturbation function top-level `_perturb_profile(a, Bmat, rng, n, sigma)`:
- no closures.
```
def _perturb_profile(a, Bmat, rng, n, sigma):
    a = a.copy()
    width = int(rng.integers(max(3, n//100), max(4, n//8)))
    if width > n: width = n
    start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
    a[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, width))
    if Bmat is not None and rng.random() < 0.25:
       j = int(rng.integers(0, Bmat.shape[0]))
       mix = float(rng.uniform(0.01, 0.10))
       a = (1.0 - mix)*a + mix*Bmat[j]
    # rare mirror blend
    if rng.random() < 0.05:
       a = 0.5*a + 0.5*a[::-1]
    return a
```
Then in loop clip normalize.

Need ensure `width` not negative; use `max(3, min(n, ...))`. `n//8` for n=2400=300, width up to300. Good.

Sigma schedule: `sigma = max(0.02, 0.25*(remaining_fraction) + 0.02)` maybe. Use `elapsed_frac = (time-start)/budget`; sigma = 0.25*(1-frac)+0.02. If too high, many rejected; population can still accept if better than worst. Good.

Population replace worst: Need track worst score. To avoid O(pop) each time, pop size small (10), linear fine.

`_exact_best_scale(a)`:

```
def _exact_best_scale(a):
    a = _normalize(a)
    maxa = float(a.max()) if a.size else 0.0
    scales = [1.0,10.0,100.0]
    if maxa * 1000.0 <= 1000.0*(1+1e-9): scales.append(1000.0)
    elif maxa > 0: scales.append(1000.0 / maxa)
    best_score=np.inf; best_seq=None
    for s in scales:
       try:
          arr=np.asarray(a)*float(s)
          # if any >1000 due clip, maybe use 1000/maxa
          if np.max(arr) > 1000.0:
              arr = np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0) # but clipping can change score; better recurrence
          seq=[float(x) for x in arr]
          score=float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
       except Exception:
          continue
       if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
           best_score=score; best_seq=seq
    if best_seq is None:
       # fallback scale 1
       best_seq=[float(x) for x in a]
    return best_score, best_seq
```

```
def _make_base_candidates(n, lp_u, rng):
    bases=[]
    for alpha in [0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6]:
        bases.append(_make_power(n, alpha,'left'))
        if rng.random()<0.8: bases.append(_make_power(n, alpha,'right'))
    bases.append(_make_uniform(n))
    if lp_u is not None: bases.append(_normalize(lp_u))
    # oscillatory
    freqs = [1,3,5]
    for f in freqs:
       theta = 2*np.pi*f*np.arange(n,float?)/n
       for rho in (0.5,0.9):
          v = 1.0 + rho*np.cos(theta)
          v = np.clip(v,1e-9,None); bases.append(v/v.sum())
    # random smooth
    for _ in range(6):
       x = np.arange(1,n+1,float)
       base = x**(-0.5)
       noise = rng.normal(0,0.5,n)
       # smooth moving average length 31
       k=len=21
       kern = np.ones(21)/21
       if n > 21: noise = np.convolve(noise, kern, mode='same')
       v = base * np.exp(noise)
       v = np.clip(v,1e-9,None); bases.append(v/v.sum())
    # random sparse
    for _ in range(3):
       v=np.zeros(n); M=max(100, n//3)
       idx = rng.integers(0,n,M)
       v[idx] += 1.0
       if v.sum()>0: bases.append(v/v.sum())
    # Limit to first 30 maybe
    return [ _normalize(b) for b in bases[:30] ]
```

Detailed code for `_mixture_search`:
```
def _mixture_search(bases, n, L, budget_s, seed):
    m = len(bases)
    if m == 0: return ([], None, None)
    B = np.vstack(bases)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    top = []
    best_c = np.inf; best_a = None; best_w = None
    # one-hot
    for j in range(m):
        a = B[j].copy()
        c = _fft_score(a, L)
        if c < best_c:
            best_c=c; best_a=a.copy(); best_w = np.eye(m)[j]
        _add_top(top, c, a, 40)
    # explicit pairs
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
        deltas = (0.2,0.4,0.6,0.8)
        for i in range(m):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
            for j in range(i+1,m):
                p = np.zeros(m); p[i]=1.0-p? Actually.
                for d in deltas:
                    p = np.zeros(m); p[i]=1.0-d; p[j]=d
                    a = p @ B
                    a = a / np.sum(a)
                    c = _fft_score(a,L)
                    if c < best_c: ...
                    _add_top
    # random loop
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it +=1
       p = rng.random(m)
       power = rng.choice((0.3,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0))
       p = p ** power
       ps = p.sum()
       if ps >0: p/=ps
       a = p @ B
       a = np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
       c = _fft_score(a,L)
       if c < best_c:
          best_c=c; best_a=a.copy(); best_w=p.copy()
       _add_top(top,c,a,40)
       if best_w is not None and c < best_c + 0.005 and it % 5 ==0:
           # pair refinement
           i = int(rng.integers(m)); j = int(rng.integers(m)); if i==j continue
           s = best_w[i]+best_w[j]
           if s >0:
              cur = best_w[i]/s
              cands_alpha = np.clip(np.concatenate((np.array([cur]), np.array([0.0,1.0]), cur + rng.normal(0,0.2,6))),0,1)
              for al in cands_alpha:
                 p2 = best_w.copy(); p2[i]=al*s; p2[j]=(1-al)*s
                 a2 = p2@B; normalize; c2=fft
                 if c2 < best_c: best...
                 if c2 < best_c: _add_top? maybe
    return top,best_a,best_w
```
In explicit pair, p@B each time. Need p vector. Good. The `np.eye(m)[j]` creates m*m memory once small; can simpler `best_w = np.zeros(m); best_w[j]=1.0`.

`_population_refine`:
```
def _population_refine(initial_profiles, Bmat, n, L, budget_s, seed):
   if not initial_profiles: initial_profiles=[_make_uniform(n)]
   deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s)-2.0)
   rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   pop=[]; scores=[]; top=[]
   # add initial
   for a in initial_profiles[:12]:
      a=_normalize(a); c=_fft_score(a,L); pop.append(a); scores.append(c); _add_top(top,c,a,30)
   while len(pop) < 8:
      parent = rng.integers(len(pop)) if pop else 0
      sigma=0.2
      child=_perturb_profile(pop[parent], Bmat, rng,n,sigma)
      child=_normalize(child); c=_fft_score(child,L)
      pop.append(child); scores.append(c); _add_top(top,c,child,30)
   best_idx=argmin(scores)
   best_a=pop[best_idx].copy(); best_c=scores[best_idx]
   t_start=time.monotonic()
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
      elapsed_frac = min(1.0,(time.monotonic()-t_start)/max(1.0,budget_s))
      sigma = 0.02 + 0.25*(1.0-elapsed_frac)
      # pick parent: tournament 2
      i1=int(rng.integers(len(pop))); i2=int(rng.integers(len(pop)))
      parent_i = i1 if scores[i1] <= scores[i2] else i2
      child = _perturb_profile(pop[parent_i], Bmat, rng, n, sigma)
      child = _normalize(child)
      c = _fft_score(child,L)
      # replace worst
      worst_i = int(np.argmax(scores))
      if c < scores[worst_i] or len(pop)<=1:
          pop[worst_i]=child; scores[worst_i]=c; _add_top(top,c,child,30)
      if c < best_c:
          best_c=c; best_a=child.copy()
      # occasionally add new random from pop? no
   return best_a, top
```

```
import numpy as np
import time

_TARGET_SCORE = 1.5027
_DEFAULT_N=2400
_TOP_CAP=30

def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=float)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        a = np.ones_like(a, dtype=float)
        s = float(a.size)
    return a / s

def _fft_length(n):
    L=1
    target=2*n-1
    while L < target: L <<=1
    return L
```
Could use bit length, but while fine. For n=2400, loop 14.

`_fft_score`:
```
def _fft_score(a, L):
    n = a.size
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)
    if b.size > 2*n-1:
       b = b[:2*n-1]
    if b.size ==0: return 0.0
    M = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M < 0.0: M = 0.0
    return float(n * M)
```
If b has tiny negative and all negative? max maybe 1e-20; fine.

```
def _make_uniform(n): return np.full(n,1.0/n)
```

`_make_power`:
```
def _make_power(n, alpha, side='left'):
   x=np.arange(1.0,n+1.0)
   v=x**(-float(alpha))
   if side == 'right': v=v[::-1]
   return _normalize(v)
```

`_resample_to_length`:
```
def _resample_to_length(x, m):
   x=np.asarray(x,float).ravel()
   if x.size == m: return _normalize(x)
   if x.size <=0: return _normalize(np.ones(m))
   old = np.linspace(0.0, x.size, x.size)
   new = np.linspace(0.0, m, m)
   y=np.interp(new, old, x)
   return _normalize(y)
```

LP:
```
def _solve_lp_u(base, time_limit):
   base = _normalize(base); n=base.size
   if n < 16 or n > 1536: return None # if too large, None
   try:
      import scipy.sparse as sp
      from scipy.optimize import linprog
   except Exception:
      return None
   m = 2*n -1
   idx_i = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
   idx_j = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
   rows = idx_i + idx_j
   cols = idx_j
   vals = np.repeat(base, n)
   rows_c = np.arange(m)
   cols_c = np.full(m, n)
   vals_c = -np.ones(m)
   rows_all = np.concatenate((rows, rows_c))
   cols_all = np.concatenate((cols, cols_c))
   vals_all = np.concatenate((vals, vals_c))
   try:
       A = sp.coo_array((vals_all, (rows_all, cols_all)), shape=(m, n+1)).astype(np.float64).tocsr()
       A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
       c_obj = np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[-1]=1.0
       bounds = [(0.0, None)] * (n+1)
       tl = int(min(60.0, max(1.0, float(time_limit))))
       res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.zeros(m), A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=np.array([1.0]), bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': tl, 'output_flag':0})
       if res is not None and np.any(np.isfinite(res.x)):
           u=np.asarray(res.x[:n],float)
           u=np.clip(u,0,None)
           if u.sum() >0: return u/u.sum()
       # some versions use coo_matrix
   except Exception:
       try:
          A = sp.coo_matrix((vals_all, (rows_all, cols_all)), shape=(m,n+1)).tocsr()
          ... same maybe
       except Exception:
          pass
   return None
```
Using `coo_array` newer; if not, `coo_matrix`. To avoid duplicate, try both? Simpler use `sp.coo_matrix`, widely available. Use `coo_matrix`. But if scipy version has `coo_array` only? `coo_matrix` still. Use:
```
try: A = sp.coo_array(...).tocsr()
except AttributeError: A = sp.coo_matrix(...).tocsr()
```
Then linprog once. Good.

```
try:
   res=linprog(..., method='highs', options={'time_limit':tl})
except Exception:
   try: res=linprog(..., options={'time_limit':tl})
   except: res=None
```

```
def _add_top(top, c, a, cap):
   if not np.isfinite(c): return
   top.append((float(c), a.copy()))
   if len(top) > cap:
      worst=0; worst_val=top[0][0]
      for i in range(1,len(top)):
         if top[i][0] > worst_val:
            worst=i; worst_val=top[i][0]
      top.pop(worst)
```

```
def _final_validate(seq):
    try:
       if not isinstance(seq, list) or len(seq)==0: return _make_return_sequence(_make_power(2400,0.5,'left'))
       arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=float).ravel()
       arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
       arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
       if arr.size ==0 or np.sum(arr) < 0.01:
           return _make_return_sequence(_make_power(2400,0.5,'left'))
       return [float(x) for x in arr]
    except Exception:
       return [1.0 for _ in range(2400)]
```

```python
"""
Inequality/convolution search for the given evaluator.

The evaluator clips the input to nonnegative numbers and then minimizes

    2 * n * max(full_convolution(a, a)) / (sum(a))**2.

Because the quantity is scale-invariant, the search is performed on
probability-like vectors summing to 1, using fast FFT convolutions.

The method combines:
  * structured nonnegative bases (one-sided power laws, oscillations,
    random smooth/sparse shapes, and an LP-produced "quiet" auxiliary
    vector minimizing cross-convolution with a main base),
  * randomized mixture search over these bases,
  * a population/random-block local refiner,
  * final exact evaluation with a few safe scalings.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

_TARGET_SCORE = 1.5027
_DEFAULT_N = 2400
_TOP_CAP = 30


def _normalize(a):
    """Return a finite nonnegative 1D array summing to 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        a = np.ones_like(a, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(a.size)
    return a / s


def _fft_length(n):
    """Power-of-two FFT length large enough for full self-convolution."""
    target = 2 * int(n) - 1
    L = 1
    while L < target:
        L <<= 1
    return L


def _fft_score(a, L):
    """
    Scaled maximum coefficient of a*a for probability vector a.
    This is max_b * n when sum(a) == 1.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return 0.0
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    keep = 2 * n - 1
    if b.size > keep:
        b = b[:keep]
    if b.size == 0:
        return 0.0
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(n * m)


def _make_uniform(n):
    return np.full(int(max(1, n)), 1.0 / float(max(1, n)), dtype=np.float64)


def _make_power(n, alpha, side="left"):
    x = np.arange(1.0, float(n) + 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.power(x, -float(alpha))
    if side == "right":
        v = v[::-1]
    return _normalize(v)


def _resample_to_length(x, m):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(max(1, m))
    if x.size == m:
        return _normalize(x)
    if x.size <= 0:
        return _make_uniform(m)
    old = np.linspace(0.0, float(x.size), x.size, dtype=np.float64)
    new = np.linspace(0.0, float(m), m, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.interp(new, old, x)
    return _normalize(y)


def _solve_lp_u(base, time_limit):
    """
    Solve an LP for a probability vector u minimizing max_k (base * u)_k.

    The LP is:
        min C
        s.t. (base * u)_k <= C     for all k
             sum u = 1
             u >= 0

    Returns u or None.
    """
    base = _normalize(base)
    n = int(base.size)
    if n < 32 or n > 648:
        return None

    try:
        import scipy.sparse as sp
        from scipy.optimize import linprog
    except Exception:
        return None

    m = 2 * n - 1

    try:
        i_rep = np.repeat(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64), n)
        j_rep = np.tile(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64), n)

        rows = i_rep + j_rep
        cols = j_rep.copy()
        vals = np.repeat(base, n).astype(np.float64)

        rows_c = np.arange(m, dtype=np.int64)
        cols_c = np.full(m, n, dtype=np.int64)
        vals_c = -np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)

        rows_all = np.concatenate((rows, rows_c))
        cols_all = np.concatenate((cols, cols_c))
        vals_all = np.concatenate((vals, vals_c))

        try:
            A = sp.coo_array((vals_all, (rows_all, cols_all)),
                             shape=(m, n + 1)).astype(np.float64).tocsr()
        except Exception:
            A = sp.coo_matrix((vals_all, (rows_all, cols_all)),
                              shape=(m, n + 1)).tocsr()

        A_eq = np.zeros((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
        A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0
        b_eq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

        c_obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
        c_obj[-1] = 1.0

        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * (n + 1)
        tl = int(min(60.0, max(1.0, float(time_limit))))

        res = None
        try:
            res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.zeros(m),
                          A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds,
                          method="highs",
                          options={"time_limit": tl, "output_flag": 0})
        except Exception:
            try:
                res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.zeros(m),
                              A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds,
                              options={"time_limit": tl})
            except Exception:
                res = None

        if res is not None and np.any(np.isfinite(res.x)):
            u = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
            u = np.clip(u, 0.0, None)
            if np.isfinite(u.sum()) and u.sum() > 0.0:
                return u / u.sum()
    except Exception:
        pass

    return None


def _add_top(top, c, a, cap=_TOP_CAP):
    """Maintain a small list of best (score, array) candidates."""
    if not np.isfinite(c):
        return
    top.append((float(c), np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()))
    if len(top) > cap:
        worst = 0
        worst_val = top[0][0]
        for i in range(1, len(top)):
            if top[i][0] > worst_val:
                worst = i
                worst_val = top[i][0]
        top.pop(worst)


def _make_base_candidates(n, lp_u, rng):
    """Create a compact bank of structured nonnegative bases."""
    n = int(max(8, n))
    bases = []

    # Main one-sided power laws and reversed variants.
    for alpha in (0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.60):
        bases.append(_make_power(n, alpha, "left"))
        if rng.random() < 0.85:
            bases.append(_make_power(n, alpha, "right"))

    # Uniform reference.
    bases.append(_make_uniform(n))

    # LP-derived "quiet" vector, if available.
    if lp_u is not None:
        u = np.asarray(lp_u, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if u.size != n:
            u = _resample_to_length(u, n)
        if u.size == n and np.sum(u) > 0.0:
            bases.append(_normalize(u))

    # Nonnegative oscillatory profiles.
    for f in (1, 3, 5):
        theta = 2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        for rho in (0.5, 0.9):
            v = np.clip(1.0 + rho * np.cos(theta), 1e-9, None)
            bases.append(_normalize(v))

    # Smoothed-noise perturbations of a 1/sqrt(i) right profile.
    for _ in range(6):
        x = np.arange(1.0, float(n) + 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
        base = np.power(x, -0.5)
        noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, n)
        if n > 21:
            kernel = np.ones(21, dtype=np.float64) / 21.0
            noise = np.convolve(noise, kernel, mode="same")
        else:
            kernel = np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n))
            noise = np.convolve(noise, kernel, mode="same")
        v = np.clip(base * np.exp(noise), 1e-9, None)
        bases.append(_normalize(v))

    # Sparse random mass distributions.
    for _ in range(3):
        v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        M = max(10, min(n, n // 3))
        idx = rng.integers(0, n, M)
        v[idx] += 1.0
        if v.sum() > 0.0:
            bases.append(_normalize(v))

    # Cap size to keep mixture search inexpensive.
    return [_normalize(b) for b in bases[:30]]


def _mixture_search(bases, n, L, budget_s, seed):
    """
    Stochastic search over probability mixtures of bases.
    Returns (top_candidates, best_profile, best_weights).
    """
    if len(bases) == 0:
        return [], None, None

    B = np.vstack([_normalize(b) for b in bases]).astype(np.float64)
    m = B.shape[0]
    n = int(n)

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    top = []
    best_c = np.inf
    best_a = None
    best_w = None

    def eval_profile(a):
        a = _normalize(a)
        return _fft_score(a, L), a

    # Single bases.
    for j in range(m):
        a = B[j]
        c, a_n = eval_profile(a)
        if c < best_c:
            best_c = c
            best_a = a_n.copy()
            best_w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
            best_w[j] = 1.0
        _add_top(top, c, a_n)

    # Explicit pairwise interpolations.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
        deltas = (0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.60, 0.80, 0.90)
        for i in range(m):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            for j in range(i + 1, m):
                for d in deltas:
                    p = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
                    p[i] = 1.0 - d
                    p[j] = d
                    a = p @ B
                    c, a_n = eval_profile(a)
                    if c < best_c:
                        best_c = c
                        best_a = a_n.copy()
                        best_w = p.copy()
                    _add_top(top, c, a_n)

    # Random Dirichlet-like mixtures, with occasional pair polishing.
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        p = rng.random(m)
        transform = float(rng.choice((0.3, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0)))
        p = np.power(p, transform)
        s = float(p.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            p = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) / float(m)
        else:
            p /= s

        a = p @ B
        c, a_n = eval_profile(a)

        if c < best_c:
            best_c = c
            best_a = a_n.copy()
            best_w = p.copy()
        _add_top(top, c, a_n)

        # Occasionally polish the best mixture around one coordinate pair.
        if best_w is not None and it % 7 == 0 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
            i = int(rng.integers(m))
            j = int(rng.integers(m))
            if i != j:
                s = float(best_w[i] + best_w[j])
                if s > 1e-12:
                    cur = float(best_w[i] / s)
                    if not np.isfinite(cur) or cur < 0.0 or cur > 1.0:
                        cur = 0.5
                    alphas = np.array([0.0, 1.0, cur], dtype=np.float64)
                    alphas = np.concatenate((
                        alphas,
                        np.clip(cur + rng.normal(0.0, 0.22, 5), 0.0, 1.0)
                    ))
                    for al in alphas:
                        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                            break
                        p2 = best_w.copy()
                        p2[i] = al * s
                        p2[j] = (1.0 - al) * s
                        a2 = p2 @ B
                        c2, a2_n = eval_profile(a2)
                        if c2 < best_c:
                            best_c = c2
                            best_a = a2_n.copy()
                            best_w = p2.copy()
                        _add_top(top, c2, a2_n)

    return top, best_a, best_w


def _perturb_profile(a, Bmat, rng, n, sigma):
    """Create a randomized child of a profile."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = int(n)

    minw = max(1, min(n, n // 100))
    maxw = max(minw, min(n, n // 8))
    if maxw <= minw:
        maxw = minw
    if maxw > n:
        maxw = n
    width = int(rng.integers(max(1, minw), maxw + 1))
    start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
    a[start:start + width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), width))

    if Bmat is not None and Bmat.size > 0 and rng.random() < 0.22:
        j = int(rng.integers(Bmat.shape[0]))
        mix = float(rng.uniform(0.01, 0.10))
        a = (1.0 - mix) * a + mix * Bmat[j]

    if rng.random() < 0.05:
        a = 0.5 * a + 0.5 * a[::-1]

    return a


def _population_refine(initial_profiles, Bmat, n, L, budget_s, seed):
    """
    Population-style random block refinement in the full nonnegative space.
    Returns (best_profile, top_candidates).
    """
    n = int(max(8, n))
    if not initial_profiles:
        initial_profiles = [_make_uniform(n)]

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    pop = []
    scores = []
    top = []

    for a in initial_profiles[:12]:
        a = _normalize(a)
        c = _fft_score(a, L)
        pop.append(a)
        scores.append(float(c))
        _add_top(top, c, a)

    while len(pop) < 8 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        parent = pop[int(rng.integers(len(pop)))]
        child = _perturb_profile(parent, Bmat, rng, n, 0.18)
        child = _normalize(child)
        c = _fft_score(child, L)
        pop.append(child)
        scores.append(float(c))
        _add_top(top, c, child)

    if not pop:
        return _make_uniform(n), top

    best_idx = int(np.argmin(scores))
    best_a = pop[best_idx].copy()
    best_c = float(scores[best_idx])

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        frac = min(1.0, (time.monotonic() - t0) / max(1.0, float(budget_s)))
        sigma = 0.02 + 0.25 * (1.0 - frac)

        i1 = int(rng.integers(len(pop)))
        i2 = int(rng.integers(len(pop)))
        parent_i = i1 if scores[i1] <= scores[i2] else i2

        child = _perturb_profile(pop[parent_i], Bmat, rng, n, sigma)
        child = _normalize(child)
        c = _fft_score(child, L)

        if len(pop) == 1:
            worst = 0
        else:
            worst = int(np.argmax(scores))

        if c < scores[worst]:
            pop[worst] = child
            scores[worst] = float(c)
            _add_top(top, c, child)

        if c < best_c:
            best_c = float(c)
            best_a = child.copy()

    return best_a, top


def _make_return_sequence(a, scale=None):
    """Convert an internal probability vector into a safe returned list."""
    a = _normalize(a)
    maxa = float(np.max(a)) if a.size else 0.0
    if scale is None:
        if maxa <= 1.0 or maxa <= 1e-12:
            scale = 1000.0
        else:
            scale = 1000.0 / maxa
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64) * float(scale)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if arr.size == 0 or float(np.sum(arr)) < 0.01:
        arr = np.ones(2400, dtype=np.float64) * 10.0

    return [float(x) for x in arr]


def _final_validate(seq):
    """Ensure the final object is a valid Python list as accepted by evaluator."""
    try:
        if not isinstance(seq, list) or len(seq) == 0:
            return _make_return_sequence(_make_power(_DEFAULT_N, 0.5, "left"))
        arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if arr.size == 0 or float(np.sum(arr)) < 0.01:
            return _make_return_sequence(_make_power(_DEFAULT_N, 0.5, "left"))
        return [float(x) for x in arr]
    except Exception:
        return [1.0 for _ in range(_DEFAULT_N)]


def _exact_best_scale(a):
    """
    Exact evaluator on a small set of safe scales.
    Returns (score, best_return_list).
    """
    a = _normalize(a)
    maxa = float(np.max(a)) if a.size else 0.0

    scales = [1.0, 10.0, 100.0]
    if maxa > 0.0:
        if maxa <= 1.0:
            scales.append(1000.0)
        else:
            scales.append(1000.0 / maxa)

    best_score = np.inf
    best_seq = None

    for s in scales:
        s = float(s)
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            continue
        arr = a * s
        if arr.size == 0:
            continue
        if float(np.max(arr)) > 1000.0 * (1.0 + 1e-9):
            continue
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)

        seq = [float(x) for x in arr]
        try:
            score = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        except Exception:
            score = np.inf

        if best_seq is None:
            best_seq = seq
        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_seq = seq

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _make_return_sequence(a)

    return float(best_score), best_seq


def _choose_length(budget_s):
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or budget_s < 30.0:
        return 512
    if budget_s < 120.0:
        return 1024
    return _DEFAULT_N


def propose_candidate(*args, **kwargs):
    """Search for a low-score nonnegative coefficient sequence."""
    # Parse budget / seed loosely.
    budget_s = 2400.0
    if "budget_s" in kwargs:
        budget_s = kwargs["budget_s"]
    else:
        nums = []
        for a in args:
            if isinstance(a, (int, float)) and not isinstance(a, bool):
                nums.append(a)
        if len(nums) >= 2:
            budget_s = nums[-1]

    seed = kwargs.get("seed", 20240525)
    if "seed" not in kwargs and args and isinstance(args[0], (int, np.integer)) \
            and not isinstance(args[0], bool):
        seed = args[0]

    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget_s) or budget_s <= 0.0:
        budget_s = 2400.0

    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 20240525

    n = _choose_length(budget_s)
    L = _fft_length(n)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    hard_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, budget_s - 5.0)

    # Optional LP-based auxiliary vector.
    lp_u = None
    lp_budget = min(45.0, max(3.0, (hard_deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.05))
    if budget_s >= 60.0 and lp_budget > 1.0:
        n_lp = min(n, 512)
        base_lp = _make_power(n_lp, 0.5, "left")
        lp_u = _solve_lp_u(base_lp, lp_budget)
        if lp_u is not None and lp_u.size != n:
            lp_u = _resample_to_length(lp_u, n)

    bases = _make_base_candidates(n, lp_u, rng)

    mixture_budget = min(360.0, max(8.0, (hard_deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.22))
    top_mix, best_mix, _ = _mixture_search(bases, n, L, mixture_budget, seed + 1)

    Bmat = np.vstack([_normalize(b) for b in bases]).astype(np.float64)

    initial_profiles = []
    if best_mix is not None:
        initial_profiles.append(best_mix)
    for _, a in top_mix[:8]:
        initial_profiles.append(a)
    initial_profiles.append(_make_power(n, 0.5, "left"))
    initial_profiles.append(_make_uniform(n))

    remaining = max(0.0, hard_deadline - time.monotonic())
    refine_budget = min(1650.0, max(10.0, remaining - 75.0))
    best_ref, top_ref = _population_refine(
        initial_profiles, Bmat, n, L, refine_budget, seed + 2
    )

    candidates = []
    if best_ref is not None:
        candidates.append(best_ref)
    if best_mix is not None:
        candidates.append(best_mix)

    for _, a in top_ref[:10]:
        candidates.append(a)
    for _, a in top_mix[:10]:
        candidates.append(a)

    candidates.append(_make_power(n, 0.5, "left"))
    candidates.append(_make_uniform(n))

    best_seq = None
    best_exact = np.inf

    # Exact-stage time guard.
    exact_guard = time.monotonic() + max(5.0, min(90.0, 0.05 * budget_s))

    seen = 0
    for a in candidates:
        if time.monotonic() > exact_guard - 3.0:
            break
        seen += 1
        if seen > 24:
            break
        try:
            score, seq = _exact_best_scale(a)
            if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_exact:
                best_exact = score
                best_seq = seq
        except Exception:
            pass

    if best_seq is None and best_ref is not None:
        _, best_seq = _exact_best_scale(best_ref)
    elif best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _make_return_sequence(_make_power(n, 0.5, "left"))

    if best_exact < _TARGET_SCORE:
        print(f"achieved exact score {best_exact:.9f} below target {_TARGET_SCORE}")
    else:
        print(f"best exact score found {best_exact if np.isfinite(best_exact) else float('inf'):.9f}")

    return _final_validate(best_seq)
```
